is such that you would put the mixture of experts where the FFN is, just basically here.

### [00:20:36.850 - 00:20:38.110]

### [00:20:38.990 - 00:20:55.410]

So one challenge that people have is when they train these MOE-based models to make sure that all experts are, I guess, having a weight, are being used.

### [00:20:55.410 - 00:21:11.750]

Because it's very possible that you train your model, and that somehow only, I don't know, one or two experts always get activated, and the other one, they always are inactive, they're never involved in the computation.

### [00:21:11.750 - 00:21:16.950]

So this problem is called routing collapse.

### [00:21:17.490 - 00:21:18.930]

So why is it called routing collapse?

### [00:21:19.290 - 00:21:25.630]

It's because a router always chooses some experts, but not others.

### [00:21:25.630 - 00:21:41.590]

So this is a challenge, and the way people try to mitigate this challenge is by changing the loss function and adding it some extra term, which is written here.

### [00:21:41.590 - 00:22:02.790]

So it's basically some hyperparameter alpha times the number of experts times the sum of quantities that depend on whether or not tokens went to a certain expert's eye and then summed over all experts.

### [00:22:02.790 - 00:22:11.030]

So it's not super important that you completely understand exactly how that formula works.

### [00:22:11.610 - 00:22:26.650]

The only thing that I think you should take away from this slide is that this extra loss allows these quantities to converge more towards uniform distributions.

### [00:22:27.410 - 00:22:28.290]

So what are these quantities?

### [00:22:28.390 - 00:22:29.130]

Just as a reminder.

### [00:22:29.130 - 00:22:43.840]

So F of i is the fraction of tokens that are routed to expert eye, and P of i is the average routing probability for expert eye.

### [00:22:45.180 - 00:22:57.580]

So when I say that all experts should be used kind of the same, what I'm saying is I want this probability to be kind of uniform across experts.

### [00:23:35.740 - 00:23:40.620]

### [00:23:40.620 - 00:24:02.560]

And I guess what I want you to remember is that this incentivizes the probability, I guess, the choice of the router to be more uniform across experts, which is something that mitigates this routing collapse phenomenon.

### [00:40:16.380 - 00:40:18.040]

obtain the highest probability sequence.

We want to figure out what the next token is.

So let's suppose we have here in this example, very basic example, like three tokens.

### [00:42:12.480 - 00:42:19.800]

And let's suppose the two highest probable tokens are A and V.

### [00:42:19.800 - 00:42:27.260]

So if we have k equal to two, what we're going to do is to keep track of these two branches.

### [00:42:27.260 - 00:42:29.880]

So that's the first iteration.

### [00:42:29.880 - 00:42:39.640]

The second iteration is we're going to look at all the probabilities of next token prediction

### [00:42:39.640 - 00:42:40.800]

for these two tokens.

### [00:42:40.800 - 00:42:46.780]

And we're going to always save the two most probable path.

And here, for instance, let's suppose if it's like the and then fluffy and then A and cute.
